# Shared context & rules for all research agents

Written by the lead agent (Claude Opus 5.5). Every research agent (Claude Sonnet 5.5, `general-purpose`) reads this file plus its own task file.

## Context
You are a research agent supporting a hiring assignment. The final product is a mobile-first landing page (in Czech) that compares ETFs traded on NYSE for a Czech retail investor. The visitor arrives from a paid ad on mobile and gives the page only a few seconds. The page is a lead magnet: it offers value and collects the visitor's contact in exchange. It will be judged mainly on the question "could we run paid ads to it tomorrow?". The page must use real data with cited sources, measure the full journey from arrival to form submission, and be deployed on free hosting (e.g. Vercel). Two ad versions must also be proposed. The original assignment (in Slovak) is in `C:\Users\matus\Desktop\B_project\research\assignment.md`.

Today's date is 2026-10-10.

Your job is RESEARCH ONLY. Decisions about the target segment, the lead magnet, the copy and the design will be made later by a human together with the lead agent, based on your findings.

## Rules
1. Every factual claim needs a source URL and an access date (2026-10-10). Tag each claim:
   - `VERIFIED` = you opened the page and it states this.
   - `INFERRED` = your own reasoning/extrapolation, or the source was not directly readable (e.g. only a search snippet).
   Never invent a URL, statistic, quote, ad or study. If you cannot find evidence, write "No evidence found" — that is a valid and useful result.
2. Prefer primary sources (regulators, official statistics, platform policies, issuer websites, original studies) over blog summaries. Always note the publication year; flag anything older than 2022 as possibly outdated.
3. Present OPTIONS with evidence and trade-offs. Do NOT give a final recommendation and do not pick a winner.
4. Do not fill in, sign up for, or submit any form on third-party websites, and do not create accounts. Observe forms only.
5. Verbatim quotes must be under 15 words; otherwise summarise in your own words.
6. Use WebSearch and WebFetch. Search in Czech where the topic concerns Czech users (e.g. "investování do ETF", "poplatky ETF", "kalkulačka investic", "jaké ETF koupit").
7. Write your output as ONE markdown file at the exact path given in your task file, using the Write tool. Do not create or modify any other file. Structure:
   ```
   # <Title>
   ## Summary (max 10 bullets)
   ## Findings (grouped by research question; each claim tagged [VERIFIED] or [INFERRED] with a source link)
   ## Evidence table (claim | number/detail | source URL | year | tag)
   ## Contradictions & open questions
   ## Options & trade-offs (input for later decisions — no recommendation)
   ## Sources (full list)
   ```
   Length: roughly 1500–2500 words plus tables.
8. Your final reply to the lead agent must contain only: the file path, a 5-bullet summary, and the 3 claims you are least confident about (so they can be checked first).
````

# Agent A — Audience: the Czech retail ETF investor

Output file: `C:\Users\matus\Desktop\B_project\research\01-audience.md`

## Research questions
1. **Size and demographics.** How many Czechs invest, and how many in ETFs specifically? Age, gender, income, education, region, investing experience. Look for: ČNB (Czech National Bank) statistics and surveys, AKAT ČR data, Česká bankovní asociace surveys, STEM / Ipsos / Median / NMS polls, data published by brokers and robo-advisors (XTB, Trading 212, Portu, Fondee, Revolut, Fio), ECB Household Finance and Consumption Survey, adoption data for the Czech DIP (dlouhodobý investiční produkt, available since 2024).
2. **Motivations.** Why do they start investing (inflation, money losing value in a bank account, pension worries, DIP tax benefit, FIRE, social media influence…)?
3. **Fears and objections.** Losing money, fees, complexity, taxes, currency risk (CZK vs USD), choosing the "right" ETF, scams, distrust of banks and advisors.
4. **Platforms.** Which brokers/apps Czech retail investors use (XTB, Trading 212, Portu, Fondee, Fio e-Broker, Revolut, Interactive Brokers, Degiro, others) and why. Market shares or client numbers if published.
5. **Comparison triggers.** What makes them look for an ETF comparison or "which ETF to buy"? Which decisions are they stuck on (S&P 500 vs. world, accumulating vs. distributing, TER, broker choice, CZK vs. USD, US vs. European-domiciled ETFs)?
6. **Voice of customer.** The exact words and phrases Czechs use when talking about ETFs, fees, S&P 500, taxes, "časový test", brokers, beginners' doubts. Sources: Czech-language subreddits (find which exist and are active), public Facebook group posts, forums (mesec.cz, finance.cz, kurzy.cz discussions), comments under popular Czech finance YouTube videos, Q&A sections of Czech finance media. Collect 20–40 short phrases (each under 15 words) with source, grouped by theme: fear, desire, confusion, jargon. Note in particular whether they talk about US tickers (VOO, SPY, QQQ…) or European UCITS tickers (CSPX, VWCE, SXR8…), since the page is about NYSE-listed ETFs.
7. **Mobile & paid-social behaviour.** How this audience uses Instagram, Facebook, TikTok, YouTube; device split; any Czech or EU data on attention/behaviour on landing pages reached from ads.
8. **Plausible sub-segments** (e.g. beginner with savings sitting in a bank account; DIY investor already on XTB/Trading 212 who wants to optimise; parent saving for children; 40+ worried about pension). For each: evidence on size, reachability via ads, and what they would value. Present as options only.
```

# Agent B — Competitor teardown

Output file: `C:\Users\matus\Desktop\B_project\research\02-competitors.md`

## Selection
- Choose **5 successful Czech or Slovak websites** in retail investing that try to win contacts or sign-ups from would-be ETF investors. Candidates to evaluate (you may pick others): Portu, Fondee, Finax, XTB CZ landing pages, Trading 212 CZ, Czech finance media / educators with an e-book, calculator, course or newsletter lead magnet (e.g. Investiční web, Peníze.cz, Finparáda, Bohatství.cz or similar). Justify why each counts as "successful" (traffic, clients, AUM, awards, visible ad spend) with sources.
- Add **3 best-in-class international references** for ETF comparison or finance lead-generation tools (candidates: justETF, Morningstar, ETF.com, NerdWallet, extraETF, Finanzfluss — choose and justify).

## For each of the 8 sites
- URL of the specific page analysed (prefer pages that receive ad traffic, e.g. dedicated campaign landing pages or pages linked from their ads, if findable).
- **First screen on mobile:** headline, subheadline, main CTA text, what is above the fold. (You read HTML/text, not the rendering — say when you are inferring layout.)
- **Value offered in exchange for the contact** (lead magnet) and whether the value is shown before the form.
- **Moment of the ask:** where/when the contact form appears (immediately, after an interaction, after scrolling, popup, exit-intent, gated result).
- **Form:** number of fields, which fields, consent checkboxes, double opt-in hints.
- **Interactive elements** (calculators, comparisons, quizzes, portfolio builders) and how each leads to the form.
- **Trust signals** (regulation/licences, client numbers, reviews, media logos, team faces).
- **Disclaimers and risk warnings.**
- **What is promised after submission.**
- **Tone and visual style** (formal/informal, "ty" vs. "vy" addressing, emoji, colours, imagery).
- **Notable weaknesses.**

## Then
- A comparison matrix (table) across all 8.
- Recurring patterns.
- Gaps nobody covers well — especially anything related to US/NYSE-listed ETFs for Czech investors.

## Notes
WebFetch may not execute JavaScript. If a page is JS-rendered and you cannot read it, say so explicitly and use alternative evidence (other pages of the same site, search snippets, reviews), tagged accordingly. Never submit any form.
```

# Agent C — Conversion & psychology evidence

Output file: `C:\Users\matus\Desktop\B_project\research\03-conversion-psychology.md`

Goal: evidence on what turns mobile visitors from paid social ads into leads, ideally for finance/investing.

Rate the evidence strength of every claim:
- **Strong** = peer-reviewed study, large-sample experiment, or multi-company dataset.
- **Medium** = credible industry dataset or a documented A/B test with numbers.
- **Weak** = opinion or vendor blog without data.

## Research questions
1. **Lead-magnet types** (calculator, quiz, comparison tool, report/e-book, checklist, email course, personalised result) — what evidence exists on their conversion and on lead quality?
2. **Gated vs. "show value first, gate the full/personalised result"** — evidence on when to ask for the contact (upfront vs. after interaction), including foot-in-the-door / commitment-consistency, sunk effort, IKEA effect, endowment with personalised results.
3. **Forms:** number of fields, single- vs. multi-step forms, inline vs. popup/modal, email-only vs. name+email, phone fields, effect of consent checkboxes (any GDPR-era evidence), button copy.
4. **Message match** between ad and landing page — evidence on effect.
5. **First screen / 5-second principles on mobile:** what must be above the fold, headline clarity, visual hierarchy, sticky CTA, thumb zone, page weight and load time effects on conversion (e.g. Google/Deloitte "Milliseconds make millions", web.dev case studies).
6. **Trust in finance:** effect of data sources, regulatory statements, social proof, faces, reviews, security badges on conversion.
7. **Relevant biases** for this context with evidence: loss aversion and fee framing (e.g. showing fees as CZK lost over 20 years vs. % TER), anchoring, default effects, scarcity/urgency (and their risks in finance), choice overload when comparing many funds.
8. **Post-submit experience:** instant delivery vs. delayed, thank-you page as a second conversion step, welcome email open rates, double opt-in effect on list quality.
9. **Benchmarks:** median landing-page conversion rate for finance / financial services (e.g. Unbounce Conversion Benchmark Report), lead conversion from Meta/TikTok traffic in finance, cost per lead ranges in CZ/CEE if available, email open/click rates for finance newsletters. Give ranges and sample sizes; note the year.
10. **Czech copy considerations:** any evidence on "tykání" vs. "vykání" in Czech marketing (especially finance) and on Czech consumers' response to aggressive vs. sober sales tone.

Where to look: Unbounce, CXL, Baymard Institute, Nielsen Norman Group, VWO / Optimizely / GoodUI case libraries, HubSpot research, web.dev case studies, Google research, academic literature (Google Scholar: interactive content, calculators, personalised feedback, loss framing of fees, foot-in-the-door online). Document A/B tests with their reported numbers and sample sizes when available.
```

```
# Agent D — Ads & compliance

Output file: `C:\Users\matus\Desktop\B_project\research\04-ads-compliance.md`

## Research questions
1. **Ad platform policies** for financial products & services when targeting the Czech Republic / EU:
   - Meta (Facebook/Instagram): financial products and services policy, special ad categories and their targeting restrictions in the EU, advertiser verification, prohibited claims.
   - Google Ads: Financial services verification — is it required for Czechia? What content triggers it?
   - TikTok: financial services ad policy for CZ.
2. **Meta Instant Forms vs. an external landing page** — only facts that matter for us (the assignment requires a landing page).
3. **Regulation of marketing communications for investment products** (Czech/EU): MiFID II Art. 24(3) and Delegated Regulation (EU) 2017/565 Art. 44 (fair, clear, not misleading; rules on past performance — e.g. 5-year periods, currency warnings, simulated performance), ESMA guidelines on marketing communications (under the cross-border distribution regulation), ESMA/ČNB statements on finfluencers (2023–2025), ČNB stance. Key question: would a lead-magnet page that compares ETFs (published by a non-broker) count as "investment advice" or an "investment recommendation" (MAR), and what wording keeps it informational? Does a non-licensed publisher need any licence or registration? Present facts and options, no recommendation.
4. **Disclaimers in Czech:** risk warning, past performance, currency risk, "not investment advice" — find real examples from Czech sites and quote short fragments (<15 words).
5. **GDPR for lead forms:** legal basis (performance of a request vs. consent; marketing consent separate), Czech ÚOOÚ guidance, required privacy information, double opt-in practice, consent wording examples.
6. **Czech cookie law:** Act 127/2005 Coll. as amended effective 1 Jan 2022 (opt-in). Impact on analytics and ad pixels: which tools/approaches may run without consent (cookieless analytics, server-side events, Google Consent Mode v2), what must wait for consent. Practical effect on measuring the funnel.
7. **Competitor ads:** use the Meta Ad Library (https://www.facebook.com/ads/library/) for Portu, Fondee, Finax, XTB, Trading 212 and Czech finance educators. Record active ad angles, hooks, formats, CTAs, and which landing pages they point to. The Ad Library is JS-heavy — if you cannot access it via WebFetch, state that clearly and use other evidence (press about their campaigns, marketing case studies, Google Ads Transparency Center). Never fabricate ad content.
8. **Ad creative and copy patterns** that perform for finance on Meta/TikTok (hooks, UGC vs. static, carousels, numbers in headlines, compliance-safe wording) — with evidence strength (Strong / Medium / Weak).
```

**→ Write** `C:\Users\matus\Desktop\B_project\research\prompts\05-data-facts.md`

```
# Agent E — Data sources & product facts

Output file: `C:\Users\matus\Desktop\B_project\research\05-data-facts.md`

Flag every fact that changed during 2024–2026.

## Research questions
1. **Accessibility.** Can a Czech retail investor actually buy US-domiciled ETFs traded on NYSE / NYSE Arca (e.g. SPY, VOO, VTI, SCHD)? Explain the PRIIPs Regulation (EU) No 1286/2014 KID requirement and its practical effect. Which brokers available to Czechs block or allow it (XTB, Trading 212, Interactive Brokers, Degiro, Fio e-Broker, Revolut, Saxo, Lynx, Portu/Fondee) and what are the routes around it (MiFID professional client status, options exercise, US ETFs that publish a KID)? Use broker help pages as sources.
2. **What "NYSE" means for ETFs.** NYSE vs. NYSE Arca vs. NYSE American; where the big ETFs are actually listed (e.g. SPY, VOO, IVV, VTI, QQQ, SCHD). Find official listing data.
3. **UCITS equivalents** of popular US ETFs (e.g. VOO/IVV → CSPX/SXR8/VUSA, VT → VWCE, QQQ → EQQQ/CNDX, SCHD → ?) with TER, domicile, distribution policy — from issuer pages or justETF.
4. **Data sources for a comparison of NYSE-listed ETFs.** For each source give: URL; fields available (ticker, name, issuer, expense ratio, AUM, inception, performance 1y/3y/5y/10y, dividend yield, distribution policy, holdings count, top holdings, index); update frequency; access method (CSV/JSON/API/manual); free-tier limits; and terms of use regarding display/redistribution on a public website. Candidates: issuer sites (iShares/BlackRock, Vanguard, State Street SPDR, Schwab, Invesco) incl. downloadable fact sheets; SEC EDGAR (prospectus fee tables, N-PORT, N-CEN); NYSE listings directory; Nasdaq Trader symbol directory (`otherlisted.txt` with exchange codes); Stooq CSV; Alpha Vantage; Financial Modeling Prep; Twelve Data; Tiingo; Yahoo Finance (unofficial, check ToS). Prefer sources that can be cited per data point.
5. **Costs for a Czech investor buying US ETFs:** commissions and CZK→USD FX conversion fees of brokers commonly used by Czechs (XTB, Trading 212, Interactive Brokers, Degiro, Fio, Revolut, Lynx), from official price lists, with the date of the price list.
6. **Czech tax facts (2026)** for ETF investors: 3-year time test for securities and the 40 million CZK cap introduced in 2025; the 100 000 CZK annual gross-sales exemption; US dividend withholding 15% with W-8BEN vs. 30% without; Czech tax on foreign dividends and foreign tax credit; accumulating vs. distributing ETFs; DIP (dlouhodobý investiční produkt) rules. Primary sources: zákon č. 586/1992 Sb. (§4, §8, §10), Finanční správa, IRS, US–CZ tax treaty.
7. **ČNB FX rates** (CZK/USD): official text-file/API URL, format, update time, terms of use.
```

**→ Write** `C:\Users\matus\Desktop\B_project\scripts\export_ai_log.py`

````
"""Render Claude Code session transcripts (JSONL) as readable Markdown in ai-log/.

Usage:
  python -I scripts/export_ai_log.py <transcript.jsonl>     # manual
  python -I scripts/export_ai_log.py < hook-input.json      # from a Stop/SubagentStop hook

Writes ai-log/<date>_session-<id>.md for the main conversation and
ai-log/<date>_session-<id>_agents/<agent>.md for every subagent. Thinking
blocks are omitted; long tool inputs/results are truncated.
"""
import json
import re
import sys
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
OUT = ROOT / "ai-log"

TOOL_INPUT_LIMIT = 4000
TOOL_RESULT_LIMIT = 1200
EMAIL_RE = re.compile(r"[\w.+-]+@gmail\.com")
SYSTEM_REMINDER_RE = re.compile(r"", re.S)


def clean(text):
    text = SYSTEM_REMINDER_RE.sub("", text)
    return EMAIL_RE.sub("[email]", text).strip()


def cut(text, limit):
    text = clean(text)
    if len(text) <= limit:
        return text
    return text[:limit] + f"\n… [truncated, {len(text) - limit} more chars]"


def fence(text):
    ticks = "````" if "```" in text else "```"
    return f"{ticks}\n{text}\n{ticks}"


def render_tool_use(block):
    name = block.get("name", "?")
    inp = block.get("input") or {}
    if name == "Agent":
        head = f"**→ Agent** ({inp.get('subagent_type', 'general-purpose')}, model: {inp.get('model', 'inherit')}) — {inp.get('description', '')}"
        return head + "\n\n" + fence(cut(inp.get("prompt", ""), TOOL_INPUT_LIMIT))
    if name in ("Write",):
        body = cut(inp.get("content", ""), TOOL_INPUT_LIMIT)
        return f"**→ Write** `{inp.get('file_path', '')}`\n\n" + fence(body)
    if name == "Edit":
        return f"**→ Edit** `{inp.get('file_path', '')}`\n\n" + fence(
            cut(f"- {inp.get('old_string', '')}\n+ {inp.get('new_string', '')}", TOOL_INPUT_LIMIT)
        )
    if name in ("Bash", "PowerShell"):
        desc = inp.get("description", "")
        return f"**→ {name}** — {desc}\n\n" + fence(cut(inp.get("command", ""), TOOL_INPUT_LIMIT))
    if name == "Read":
        return f"**→ Read** `{inp.get('file_path', '')}`"
    if name == "WebSearch":
        return f"**→ WebSearch** `{inp.get('query', '')}`"
    if name == "WebFetch":
        return f"**→ WebFetch** {inp.get('url', '')}" + (
            f"\n\n> prompt: {cut(inp.get('prompt', ''), 400)}" if inp.get("prompt") else ""
        )
    return f"**→ {name}**\n\n" + fence(cut(json.dumps(inp, ensure_ascii=False, indent=1), 1500))


def result_text(block):
    content = block.get("content")
    if isinstance(content, str):
        return content
    parts = []
    for c in content or []:
        if c.get("type") == "text":
            parts.append(c.get("text", ""))
        else:
            parts.append(f"[{c.get('type')}]")
    return "\n".join(parts)


def render(jsonl_path, title):
    lines = [f"# {title}", ""]
    for raw in Path(jsonl_path).read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
        try:
            entry = json.loads(raw)
        except json.JSONDecodeError:
            continue
        kind = entry.get("type")
        msg = entry.get("message") or {}
        content = msg.get("content")
        stamp = (entry.get("timestamp") or "")[:19].replace("T", " ")

        if kind == "user":
            if entry.get("isMeta"):
                text = content if isinstance(content, str) else result_text({"content": content})
                first = clean(text).splitlines()[0] if clean(text) else ""
                lines += [f"*[context injected: {first[:120]}]*", ""]
                continue
            if isinstance(content, str):
                text = clean(content)
                if text:
                    lines += [f"## 👤 User · {stamp}", "", text, ""]
                continue
            for block in content or []:
                if block.get("type") == "tool_result":
                    text = cut(result_text(block), TOOL_RESULT_LIMIT)
                    if text:
                        lin
… [truncated, 2253 more chars]
````

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 13:03:37

The export is set up and now runs automatically. The hook should regenerate `ai-log/` after every turn and whenever a research agent finishes. I haven't seen it fire in this session yet: the hook settings folder didn't exist when the session started, and the app may only load it in a new one. I'll check when the first research agent finishes, and until it's confirmed I'll run the export myself before each commit.

**How it works**
- `/export` is a command only you can type, so I used the app's own session export instead. That export also includes the transcripts of all subagents, which `/export` doesn't.
- [scripts/export_ai_log.py](scripts/export_ai_log.py) reads the live session files and writes readable Markdown:
  - [ai-log/2026-10-10_session-66014a9f.md](ai-log/2026-10-10_session-66014a9f.md) holds the main conversation: your messages, my replies, and every tool call.
  - `ai-log/2026-10-10_session-66014a9f_agents/` has one file per research agent, showing its prompt, searches, pages fetched and results.
  - Internal reasoning is left out, long tool results are shortened, any `@gmail.com` address is replaced with `[email]`, and the system notes the app adds to messages are stripped.
- The hook lives in `.claude/settings.local.json`, which is gitignored so people who clone the repo don't get it. You can turn it off by deleting that file.
- I tested it with simulated hook input and it exports correctly. It's committed as `da7c0f4`.
- The app also saved a zip of the raw session at `C:\Users\matus\Downloads\session-export-1791637293206.zip`. It isn't in the repo, and you can delete it.

The five research agents are still running.

1	# Conversion & psychology evidence for a mobile finance lead-gen landing page
2	
3	All sources accessed 2026-10-10. Tags: `VERIFIED` = I opened the page (or extracted the PDF) and it states this; `INFERRED` = my reasoning, or I only saw a search-result snippet / secondary summary. Evidence strength: **Strong** = peer-reviewed, large-sample experiment or multi-company dataset; **Medium** = credible industry dataset or documented A/B test with numbers; **Weak** = opinion, vendor blog, or number without method. Each claim carries `[tag · strength]`.
4	
5	## Summary

```
**Experiment #6068 (The Heritage Foundation)** tested whether a GDPR-compliant explicit opt-in would affect email acquisition.

- **Control conversion rate:** 47.1%
- **Treatment (T1: GDPR Version) conversion rate:** 39.8%
- **Relative difference:** -15.4%, with 100% confidence. The article calls this a statistically significant drop.
- **Sample size:** 3,040 total. The page says the required sample was 357.
- **Test duration:** 05/10/2018 to 05/18/2018.
- **Offer:** The page doesn't specify what the offer was.
- **Consent checkbox treatment:** The treatment added a required checkbox telling visitors they would receive both the offer and future email updates. The page doesn't give the checkbox's exact wording, which appears only in an image.
```

</details>

<details><summary>result</summary>

| Metric | Single opt-in (SOI) | Double opt-in (DOI) |
|---|---|---|
| Visitor-to-subscriber conversion rate | 1.28% | 0.33% |
| Newsletter open rate (grand total) | 27.36% | 35.72% |
| Newsletter click-through rate (average) | 2.36% | 4.19% |

**Dataset and time period:** The article analyzed premium GetResponse SMB accounts from 2022 that used online subscription forms. It examined 2.76 billion individual newsletters with its Analytics team. The article doesn't say whether "2022" refers to the account cohort or the sending period, so that detail is unclear.
```

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 19:40:04

Only one of the five research reports is finished so far: the conversion-psychology one, which I've checked and committed. The other four were cut off at about 15:20 by the usage limit. After it reset I resumed each of them where it stopped, so they keep the research they'd already done, and they're running now.

**Report C check (10 claims re-opened at the source)**
- **8 confirmed.** This includes the main conversion benchmarks from Unbounce's 2024 report, which will feed the "expected conversion" section of the README:
  - general financial-services pages: 8.3%
  - investing pages: 3.9%
  - finance traffic from paid social: 9.3%
  - finance on mobile: 11.5%, vs 9% on desktop
- **2 partly right, now corrected in the report:**
  - The agent described the offer in the consent-checkbox test as "a free guide", but the source doesn't say what the offer was.
  - The agent tied "37 brands" to lead-generation sites in the page-speed study. 37 is the whole study across all industries; the number of lead-gen sites isn't given.
- **One claim it was unsure of checks out:** the choice-overload meta-analysis (63 conditions, N=5,036, average effect close to zero). The agent tagged it as inferred, which was the right call.

**Two gaps to keep in mind for our design decisions:**
- No controlled study exists on *when* to ask for the email around a calculator or quiz result.
- No finance-specific evidence exists for addressing visitors informally ("ty") vs formally ("vy"). The four Czech investing sites the agent could read all use the formal "vy".

The checks are in [research/99-verification-log.md](research/99-verification-log.md), committed as `da4a391`.

I'll check each remaining report as it comes in, then write the decision brief.

        for v in o: walk(v)

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 19:42:02

Report B (competitor teardown) is in, checked and committed. The audience, ads/compliance and ETF data/facts agents are still running.

**What I checked:** I re-opened the sources for 7 of its claims, and all 7 hold up. The only slip was trivial: XTB's form has 26 hidden tracking fields, not "about 25". One claim I couldn't check is the Similarweb traffic estimates, which need a login; they're tagged as inferred and only good as a rough size signal.

**What the competitors do:**
- **Portu, Fondee and Finax** all show a calculator first and only then ask for something small. That's an email (Fondee), an email plus password (Portu), or just your investing goal (Finax). In all three the ask is "open an account", not "get a guide".
- **XTB** is the only one running a real lead magnet, a free ETF e-book. Its form has 4 required fields (first name, surname, email and phone) plus a single required checkbox that also covers consent to marketing. On a phone the form starts below the first screen, and a warning about leveraged trading (CFDs) fills the top third of the page.
- **Mismatch in tone:** XTB's ads address people informally ("ty") while its landing pages are formal ("vy"). Portu mixes both, even within the page's opening headline.
- **Ad hooks running on Facebook/Instagram in Czechia now:** "work out what you could have in 10/20/30 years", "no hidden fees", "you don't need millions", a 1,000 CZK bonus, and "download the e-book".
- **Nobody covers our angle:** no Czech competitor explains NYSE-listed (US-domiciled) ETFs to Czech investors. Finex's screener lists SPY, VTI and QQQ, but says nothing about where they're listed, where they're domiciled, or whether a Czech retail investor can buy them.

That last point ties to the open question of whether Czech retail investors can buy these ETFs at all under the EU rules (PRIIPs). The ETF data agent is researching it with current broker sources; the competitor agent only found sources from 2018 and 2020.

Checks are in [research/99-verification-log.md](research/99-verification-log.md), committed as `ba1a501`.

Report A (audience) is checked and committed. Two of the six claims I re-opened were overstated, and I've corrected them in the report. The ads/compliance and ETF data/facts agents are still running.

**The two corrections**
- **"14% of Czechs invest in ETFs" was a misreading.** The Generali source only says ETFs keep "a stable share of around 14%" without saying of what; it most likely means a share among investors' products, not of the population. We have no reliable figure for how many Czechs hold ETFs.
- **"SPY, VOO and IVV are unavailable" was inflated.** Finex (updated 25 Sep 2026) names only SPY as its example. The core point holds: under the EU PRIIPs rules most brokers can't sell US-domiciled ETFs to retail clients. Fio (USD 7.95 per order, up to 100 shares) and Patria are the named exceptions.

**What holds up**
- **Who invests:** about 39% of Czechs say they invest. Non-investors' main barriers are fear of losing money (73%), feeling they don't have enough money (71%) and lacking know-how (71%). The main reason people invest is retirement (78%).
- **Long-term investment product (DIP):** 244,217 contracts on 31 Mar 2026, with a tax deduction of up to 48,000 CZK a year.
- **Ad reach in Czechia:** YouTube 8.08M, Facebook 5.0M, Instagram 3.95M, TikTok 2.13M adults.
- **How investors talk:** two forum quotes from 6 Sep 2026 match the source word for word. On the forum they discuss the European versions of these funds, under tickers like VWCE, IWDA, SPYL, SXR8 and CSPX.

The research keeps pointing at one problem. The brief says "ETFs traded on NYSE", but Czech retail investors mostly can't buy them and don't talk about them. How to handle that will be the first point in the decision brief.

Checks are in [research/99-verification-log.md](research/99-verification-log.md), committed as `6d13ac6`.

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 19:44:37

I've checked the ads and compliance report and committed it. Four of the five reports are now done and checked; only the ETF data and facts one is still running. Two of its findings affect "could we run ads to it tomorrow" directly.

**Can we advertise at all?**
- **Google Ads:** probably not, for an unlicensed publisher. Since 23 Jul 2026 Google requires financial-services advertisers targeting Czechia to verify against regulator registries, and "investment" and "brokerages" are covered. A site without its own licence can't apply; a licensed firm would have to apply for it.
- **TikTok:** allows investment ads in Czechia only for licensed advertisers, shown to over-18s, with disclaimers. It bans "advisory services", so the offer's wording matters.
- **Meta (Facebook/Instagram):** still unclear, and the agent and I both failed to read the policy page. Snippets say a licence is needed in the target country "where required". Competitors run ads in Czechia to everyone aged 18–65+.

**How far the content can go:** the Czech National Bank (ČNB, Jan 2025) draws the line at personalisation. Showing everyone a fee comparison or education is fine. Telling one person "this ETF suits you" counts as investment advice and needs a licence. The EU markets regulator ESMA (Jan 2026) says a "not investment advice" disclaimer does not protect you.

**Measuring the funnel:** Czech rules require consent before analytics cookies, so part of the funnel data will be missing or estimated. Nobody has published a Czech opt-in rate.

Three of the six claims I checked held fully, and the agent's uncertain Meta claim is still unconfirmed. The other two were overstated:
- **Cookie banner:** the agent wrote that the Czech data-protection authority (ÚOOÚ) "wants" the reject button in the banner's first layer. The authority calls it good practice, not a requirement. I've corrected the report.
- **Google start date:** Google's own help pages give two start dates for Czechia (23 Jul and 15 Sep 2026). Both have passed, so it doesn't change the conclusion.

Checks are in [research/99-verification-log.md](research/99-verification-log.md), committed as `a3c0a25`. When the last report lands I'll check it and write the decision brief.

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 19:47:14

Now a summary of the error patterns at the end of the log. This is what the README section on "where the AI was wrong" will be built from.

**→ Bash** — Append verification totals and commit report E

```
cat >> research/99-verification-log.md <<'EOF'

## Totals

| Report | Claims re-checked | Confirmed | Partial | Wrong | Inconclusive | Not checkable |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 01 Audience (A) | 6 | 4 | 1 | 1 | – | – |
| 02 Competitors (B) | 7 | 7 | – | – | – | 1 (Similarweb, login) |
| 03 Conversion (C) | 10 | 8 | 2 | – | – | – |
| 04 Ads & compliance (D) | 6 | 4 | 1 | – | 1 (Meta policy unreadable) | – |
| 05 Data & facts (E) | 6 | 5 | 1 | – | – | 1 (FMP ToS, 403) |
| **Total** | **35** | **28 (80%)** | **5** | **1** | **1** | **2** |

Plus one error by the lead agent itself (L1).

## Where the agents went wrong: patterns

1. **Every number was right; the qualifiers around them were not.** No re-checked figure was wrong. All 6 problems were about scope or interpretation:
   - tickers added that the source didn't name (A1);
   - an ambiguous "14% share" read as 14% of the population (A3);
   - a study-wide sample size attributed to one sub-group (C4);
   - a detail invented to fill a gap ("the offer was a free guide", C3);
   - a sub-claim merged in from elsewhere (E6);
   - guidance upgraded to a requirement (D4).
2. **The errors sat in claims tagged `VERIFIED`, not `INFERRED`.** The agents' self-reported "least confident" claims were mostly fine (C8, A6, E5) or honestly unresolved (D6). A `VERIFIED` tag means "I opened the page", not "my reading of it is correct". Interpretation is where checking pays off.
3. **Self-correction against the prompt worked.** Agent E refused a false premise in the lead agent's own prompt (L1) and cited a primary source.
4. **Access limits shaped the evidence.** Reddit, Facebook groups, YouTube comments, Meta's policy page, IBKR and Revolut help pages were not reachable by fetch. Voice of customer therefore comes from one forum and the App Store, and the Meta ad policy stays an open question.

## How this was applied
- Every PARTIAL/WRONG claim is corrected in place in the report, marked *(lead-agent check …)* with a pointer to this log.
- The decision brief (00-decision-brief.md) uses only CONFIRMED or explicitly flagged claims.
EOF
git add research/ ai-log && git commit -q -m "Research E (data & facts) + verification; verification totals and error patterns

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>" && git log --oneline | head -1

# Decision brief: NYSE ETF lead-magnet page for Czech retail investors

Synthesis of the five research reports (01–05), after the lead agent's verification ([99-verification-log.md](99-verification-log.md)). It lists **open decisions with options, evidence and trade-offs. Nothing here is decided.** Decisions are made with the human and recorded in `decisions.md`.

Reference format: `[02]` = report 02-competitors.md; `[log C1]` = row C1 of the verification log. ✔ = confirmed in verification; ⚠ = partial/inferred or contested.

---

## Fixed by the assignment (not up for decision)
Mobile-first page in Czech for paid traffic · lead magnet with value visible before the form · minimal form · real data with sources · full-funnel measurement · two ad versions · deployed on free hosting · README with section-order rationale, expected conversion, 3 ranked A/B hypotheses.

## Facts that constrain every decision

| # | Fact | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| F1 | **Most Czech retail investors cannot buy NYSE-listed ETFs.** US-domiciled ETFs have no PRIIPs KID, so DEGIRO, LYNX and Saxo block new retail purchases; IBKR, Trading 212 and XTB do too, by indirect evidence. **Fio** explicitly allows them (execution-only; USD 7.95 per order up to 100 shares + ~3% indicative FX spread vs ČNB). Finex also names **Patria**. | ✔ [05], [log E2, E5, E6, A1] |
| F2 | "NYSE" for ETFs in practice means **NYSE Arca**: SPY, VOO, IVV, VTI, VT, SCHD. QQQ is Nasdaq. NYSE main board lists only 79 ETFs; NYSE American lists 0. | ✔ [log E3] |
| F3 | Czech investors talk in **UCITS tickers** (forum mentions: VWCE 185, IWDA 141, SPYL 106, SXR8 78, CSPX 25). UCITS twins exist for S&P 500 (CSPX/SXR8/VUAA, TER 0.07%), All-World (VWCE 0.14%) and Nasdaq-100 (CNDX/EQQQ 0.30%). No exact SCHD twin. | [01], [05] |
| F4 | **Nobody in the Czech market explains NYSE/US ETFs vs their buyable twins.** Finex's screener lists SPY, VTI and QQQ with no note on listing venue, domicile or availability. | ✔ [02], [log B7] |
| F5 | **Advice line (ČNB, Jan 2025):** an individualised recommendation of a specific instrument = investment advice → needs a licence. A public, non-individualised recommendation is allowed but carries MAR duties (identity, date, sources, facts vs opinion, conflicts). A "not investment advice" disclaimer does not protect (ESMA, Jan 2026). | ✔ [log D3, D5] |
| F6 | **Ad platforms:** Google requires financial-services verification in CZ since 23 Jul 2026, and an unlicensed publisher can't apply → Google Ads is likely closed. TikTok: licensed advertisers only, "advisory services" banned. **Meta: policy text unreadable, licence "where required" — open risk.** Competitors run Meta ads to all 18–65+ in CZ. | ✔ D1, D2; ⚠ D6 |
| F7 | **Consent:** marketing e-mails need prior opt-in consent (Act 480/2004 §7). Analytics cookies need opt-in (ÚOOÚ). Sending what the visitor asked for (the result/report) does not need marketing consent. | ✔ [04], ⚠ D4 |
| F8 | **Data licensing:** every free data API checked forbids public display; issuer sites allow personal use or need permission. Free and unrestricted: SEC EDGAR, Nasdaq Trader directory, ČNB FX API, broker price lists (PDF). | [05] (FMP ⚠ E7) |
| F9 | **Benchmarks** (Unbounce 2024, medians of pages that *had* conversions → optimistic): financial services 8.3%, **investing 3.9%**, finance paid social 9.3%, finance mobile 11.5%. Quiz funnels: 7.85–11.28% lead capture (means). | ✔ [log C1, C9] |

---

| **0b NYSE → UCITS twin bridge** | Use famous US ETFs (VOO, SPY, QQQ, VT…) as the recognisable reference; for each show its buyable UCITS twin and the cost of each ro

# Decisions log

Decisions made by the human together with the lead agent, based on [00-decision-brief.md](00-decision-brief.md). Each entry records the choice, the alternatives rejected and the evidence used. This file is the source for the README rationale.

## Round 1 — 2026-10-10

| # | Decision | Chosen | Rejected | Why (evidence) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D0 | Treatment of "NYSE ETFs" | **NYSE → UCITS twin bridge, neutral framing.** Famous NYSE ETFs (VOO, SPY, …) are the recognisable reference; each is mapped to its buyable UCITS twin with the cost of each route. Framed positively as a comparison, not around "you're blocked". | Barrier-led hook (recommended by AI, overridden by human); literal NYSE-only; UCITS-only | F1 most retail brokers block US-domiciled ETFs (PRIIPs), only Fio/Patria sell them; F3 Czechs buy UCITS twins; F4 no Czech competitor maps one to the other. Human preferred a positive frame over a negative "you can't buy it" hook. |
| D1 | Target segment | **DIY investor who knows the S&P 500**: already on XTB/Trading 212/Degiro or about to start; knows tickers from YouTube/social media; cares about TER, FX and tax. | Bank-savings beginner, DIP/40+, parents | [01] forum and App Store evidence of active questions on TER, venue, FX and tax; UCITS vocabulary. Meta targeting is broad anyway, so the segment drives copy, not targeting. |
| D2 | Value for the contact | **Cost result + twin-map PDF.** The calculator shows the headline cost of the visitor's ETF route in CZK over N years *before* the form; the full breakdown plus the NYSE→UCITS map and tax/broker checklist go by e-mail. | Guide-only, quiz → shortlist (advice risk), mini-course | Fee salience changes choices (FCA OP32, Choi et al.) [03]; only Portu has a fee calculator in CZ [02]; no individualised fund recommendation → stays outside the ČNB advice line (F5). |
| D3 | Moment of the ask | **After interaction, with the partial result visible.** | Upfront hero form, optional gate | Matches the brief ("value before the form"); same pattern as Portu/Fondee/Finax [02]; no controlled test favours any timing [03], so this is a candidate A/B hypothesis. |

import sys
p=sys.argv[1]
s=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()
d7_full="| D7 | Section order | **Tool-first:** hero matching the ad, calculator starting on the first screen → partial result → form → full breakdown → how it's calculated + sources → FAQ → disclaimers. | Table-first, form-first | Consistent with D3/D4. |\n"
s=s.replace("| D7 | Section order | **Tool-first:** *(see row below)* | | |\n", d7_full)
tail="\n## Round 2 (continued)\n\n| # | Decision | Chosen | Rejected | Why / implications |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n"+d7_full
assert tail in s
s=s.replace(tail,"")
open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(s)
EOF
tail -n 12 research/decisions.md | cut -c1-120

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 20:15:13

Round 4 is in. I'll record it, then build the 5 visual previews. To keep them realistic I'll use the same Czech copy and real numbers in all five, starting with an illustrative cost calculation.

**→ Bash** — Compute illustrative cost scenario for preview mockups

```
python -I - <<'EOF'
# Illustrative scenario for previews: 100k CZK initial + 3k CZK/month, 20 years, 7% gross p.a.
usd=21.745
r=(1.07)**(1/12)-1
def fv(initial, monthly, fx_in, comm_czk, ter, fx_out, months=240):
    v=initial*(1-fx_in)-comm_czk
    paid=initial
    for m in range(months):
        v*= (1+r)*(1-ter/12)
        v+= monthly*(1-fx_in)-comm_czk
        paid+=monthly
    return v*(1-fx_out), paid
fio, paid = fv(100000,3000,0.03,7.95*usd,0.0003,0.03)
xtb, _ = fv(100000,3000,0.005,0,0.0007,0.005)
ideal,_ = fv(100000,3000,0,0,0,0)
print('paid in', round(paid))
print('no-cost', round(ideal))
print('VOO via Fio', round(fio), 'cost', round(ideal-fio))
print('CSPX via XTB', round(xtb), 'cost', round(ideal-xtb))
print('difference', round(xtb-fio))
# quarterly Fio buying instead (9000 per quarter)
EOF
```

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 20:21:06

That's useful direction. All five drifted into editorial, data-dense layouts, which is the opposite of what a 2-second mobile visit needs. I'll save it as a design rule so it carries into the build.

**→ Write** `C:\Users\matus\.claude\projects\C--Users-matus-Desktop-B-project\memory\minimal-fintech-design.md`

```
---
name: minimal-fintech-design
description: Landing page visual style must be modern minimal fintech — very little text/data per screen, built for a ~2-second attention span
metadata:
  type: feedback
---

The user rejected five visual previews (data journalism, receipt, terminal, twin circles, Swiss grid) for the ETF landing page because they were the opposite of modern minimal fintech: too much data, too much text, too much clutter.

**Why:** a visitor arriving from a mobile ad decides in ~2 seconds whether to continue or close the tab. Every extra line of text or number on the first screen costs attention.

**How to apply:** one statement + one action per screen; headline of a few words; one-tap inputs (chips) with progressive disclosure, one question per step; a single big number as the result; details, sources and disclaimers pushed below or behind a tap. Reference feel: Revolut, Wise, Robinhood, Cash App, Mercury — not editorial or dashboard layouts. Related: [[clientelo-task-b-project]]
```

metadata:
  type: project
---

Hiring assignment "Zadanie B" due **16 Oct 2026**: a mobile-first Czech lead-gen landing page comparing NYSE-listed ETFs for a Czech retail investor, deployed on free hosting, plus 2 ad versions, a README (AI usage, section-order rationale, expected conversion, 3 A/B hypotheses) and `ai-log/` transcripts. Repo: github.com/Matush77/Clientelo-task-b (public; ask before pushing).

**Why:** judged mainly on "could we run ads to it tomorrow" and on how AI was used and checked.

**How to apply:** research reports, the verification log and the decisions log live in `research/` (decisions.md is the source of truth for choices made with the user). ai-log is auto-exported by a Stop/SubagentStop hook (`scripts/export_ai_log.py`, configured in `.claude/settings.local.json`). Design style: [[minimal-fintech-design]].
```

**→ Bash** — Compute cost breakdown components for preview

```
python -I - <<'EOF'
usd=21.745
r=(1.07)**(1/12)-1
def fv(fx_in=0, comm=0, ter=0, fx_out=0, months=240, initial=100000, monthly=3000):
    v=initial*(1-fx_in)-comm
    for m in range(months):
        v*=(1+r)*(1-ter/12)
        v+=monthly*(1-fx_in)-comm
    return v*(1-fx_out)
base=fv()
def parts(fx, comm, ter):
    t=base-fv(ter=ter); f=base-fv(fx_in=fx,fx_out=fx); c=base-fv(comm=comm); tot=base-fv(fx_in=fx,comm=comm,ter=ter,fx_out=fx)
    return [round(x,-2) for x in (t,f,c,tot)]
print('Fio VOO  ter,fx,comm,total', parts(0.03,7.95*usd,0.0003))
print('XTB CSPX ter,fx,comm,total', parts(0.005,0,0.0007))
print('final value base', round(base,-2), 'XTB', round(fv(0.005,0,0.0007,0.005),-2), 'Fio', round(fv(0.03,7.95*usd,0.0003,0.03),-2))
EOF
```

final value base 1909600.0 XTB 1872500.0 Fio 1703900.0

## Round 5 — 2026-10-10 (visual direction)

| # | Decision | Chosen | Rejected | Why / implications |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D9b | Visual direction | **Minimal fintech, dark ("Noc" family):** near-black, one accent, one statement + one action per screen, 1-tap chips, one big number, bottom-sheet form. The neon lime accent is "maybe too much", so the accent is still being tuned (v3 previews). | v1 previews (all 5: data journalism, receipt, terminal, twins, Swiss grid) for being too data-heavy and cluttered for a 2-second attention span; v2 light/colour-field variants | Human design judgement: the visitor decides in ~2 seconds; less text beats more data on the first screen. Consistent with evidence that pages at a simple reading level convert better [log C2] and that only ~20–28% of words get read [03]. |
| D12 | Brand name | **Veenos**: a play on Czech "výnos" (return/yield). | "Dvojče" / "ETF dvojčata" placeholders | Short, memorable, ties to the investor's goal. |
EOF
python -I - <<'EOF'
p=r"C:\Users\matus\.claude\projects\C--Users-matus-Desktop-B-project\memory\clientelo-task-b-project.md"

open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(s)
EOF
echo done

cat >> research/decisions.md <<'EOF'

## Round 7 — 2026-10-10 (v5 feedback)

| # | Decision | Chosen | Why / implications |
|---|---|---|---|
| D13 | Hero | **Hero A ("Stejný index. Jiná cena." + one CTA) with D's three short trust checks.** "10 vteřin" removed from the sub-headline. | Human choice from v4/v5 previews. |
| D14 | Calculator layout | **Steps flow down the page:** each answered step folds into a one-line summary, the page scrolls to the next question, and the result appears below. | Human request; keeps context visible and feels like a page, not an app. |
| D15 | CTA label | **A/B test: "Začít investovat" (A) vs "Spočítat poplatky" (B).** | Lead agent flagged the risk that "Začít investovat" promises account opening while the tap opens a calculator; the human chose to test it. → One of the README A/B hypotheses. |
| D5c | Phone field (revises D5) | **Phone optional and opt-in:** name + e-mail required; a "Chci zavolat" switch reveals the phone field, which is required only when the switch is on. | Human request. Only visitors who want a call give a number, which also gives explicit consent for the one call (Act 127/2005 §96, see D5b). Lowers friction vs three mandatory fields. |
| D9d | Visual refinements | Background lifted from pure black to a soft dark grey; website header with a menu (mock); a footer; horizontal cost bars in the result; more generous spacing. | Human feedback: v5 "looks like an app". |
EOF
echo ok

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 20:57:36

v6 is ready to review ([design/previews/v6.html](design/previews/v6.html), commit `16c3e89`). The outlined phone is a working prototype: menu, calculator, form and breakdown.

**What changed from v5**
- **Background:** lifted from pure black to a soft dark grey (`#18191c`).
- **Hero:** "Stejný index. Jiná cena.", "Zjistěte, kolik vám ETF ukusuje na poplatcích." (no "10 vteřin"), then the three checks and the CTA.
- **Website feel:** a sticky header with the logo, a "Jak počítáme" link and a menu icon that opens a full-screen menu. A short "Jak počítáme" section with the sources and a footer with links and the legal disclaimer sit below the calculator.
- **Result chart:** two horizontal cost bars with values, replacing the vertical blocks.
- **Spacing:** one consistent spacing scale, more air between sections and inside cards.
- **Phone is opt-in:** only name and e-mail are required. The "Chci zavolat" switch reveals the phone field and makes it required only while it's on. The thank-you text changes to match ("Zavoláme vám jednou…"). This is also cleaner legally, because it gives explicit consent for that one call.
- **CTA A/B:** the toggle at the top of the page switches every button between "Začít investovat" and "Spočítat poplatky". It's recorded as an A/B hypothesis in [research/decisions.md](research/decisions.md).

**Open items before the build**
- **Numbers are illustrative.** They assume monthly purchases on both routes, which makes Fio's USD 7.95 commission look very heavy at small amounts. For a fair comparison (MiFID Art. 44 sets "fair and balanced" as the standard), the real calculator should probably batch the Fio orders, e.g. quarterly. That's a build-phase choice I'll put in the plan.
- **Index coverage:** only S&P 500 is wired up. "Celý svět" and "Celý trh USA" need verified fund fee (TER) data for VT and VTI first.

If v6 looks right, the next step is the build plan: stack, hosting, form backend, e-mail delivery and tracking. If not, tell me what to change.

<!doctype html>
<html lang="cs">

<style>
  * { box-sizing: border-box; margin: 0; padding: 0; }
  body { background: #e9e8e5; color: #1b1b1d; font: 15px/1.5 "Manrope", system-ui, sans-serif; padding: 40px 16px 80px; }
  .head, .block { max-width: 1280px; margin: 0 auto; }
  .head { margin-bottom: 28px; }
  .head h1 { font-weight: 800; font-size: 28px; letter-spacing: -0.03em; }
  .head p { max-width: 880px; margin-top: 8px; color: #55555b; }
  .block { margin-bottom: 56px; }
  .block h2 { font-weight: 800; font-size: 20px; letter-spacing: -0.02em; }
  .block .desc { color: #55555b; font-size: 14px; margin: 4px 0 18px; max-width: 880px; }
  .phones { display: flex; gap: 20px; flex-wrap: wrap; align-items: flex-start; }
  .cap { font-size: 12px; color: #4d4d52; text-align: center; margin-top: 8px; font-weight: 700; max-width: 320px; }
  .cap span { display: block; font-weight: 500; color: #7d7d82; }
  .ab { display: flex; gap: 8px; margin: 0 0 18px; flex-wrap: wrap; }
  .ab button { font: 700 13px "Manrope"; border: 1px solid #c9c8c4; background: #fff; border-radius: 999px; padding: 8px 14px; cursor: pointer; }
  .ab button.on { background: #18191c; color: #fff; border-color: #18191c; }
  .live { outline: 3px solid #cfe68a; outline-offset: 4px; border-radius: 44px; }

  /* ===== tokens ===== */
  .ui { --bg:#18191c; --fg:#f3f3ef; --muted:#9a9b97; --line:#2f3035; --card:#202125; --chip:#26272c; --field:#2c2d32; --accent:#cfe68a; --on-accent:#18191c; font-family: "Manrope", sans-serif; }
  .tab { font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums; }

  /* ===== A: logos ===== */
  .logos { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fill, minmax(280px, 1fr)); gap: 18px; }
  .lcard { border-radius: 22px; overflow: hidden; background: #fff; box-shadow: 0 8px 24px rgba(0,0,0,.08); }
  .lcard .dark { background: #18191c; color: #f3f3ef; height: 150px; display: grid; place-items: center; }
  .lcard .light { background: #f6f5f1; color: #18191c; height: 70px; display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; gap: 22px; border-top: 1px solid #e5e3de; }
  .lcard .meta { padding: 14px 18px 18px; }
  .lcard .meta b { font-size: 15px; display: block; }
  .lcard .meta span { font-size: 13px; color: #66666b; }
  .fav { width: 34px; height: 34px; border-radius: 9px; background: #18191c; display: grid; place-items: center; }
  .wm { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 9px; font-weight: 800; letter-spacing: -0.045em; }
  .wm.xl { font-size: 38px; gap: 12px; } .wm.md { font-size: 20px; }

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 21:11:52

v7 is ready to review ([design/previews/v7.html](design/previews/v7.html), commit `aff719d`).

**A · Logo: four options that play on "výnos"**
1. **V-šipka:** the V's right arm rises into an arrow, so it reads as return going up and also as a check mark.
2. **Složený výnos:** a lime tile with a small rising chart line.
3. **Rostoucí tečky:** the twin dots grown into three rising dots, for compound growth.
4. **Čárka nad ee:** a wordmark only. The "ee" carries a Czech čárka, because "Veenos" is how you say "výnos".

**B · Five dynamic heroes.** All share the same copy and CTA, and all stop animating when the phone has "reduce motion" on.
1. A compound-growth line draws itself behind the headline.
2. A photo of Prague Castle at dusk with a dark overlay and a slow zoom. It's free from Unsplash; for production we'd host it ourselves.
3. A floating result card where the bars fill and the number counts up, so the value shows before any tap.
4. A slow blurred glow, with the fund pair above the headline cycling between S&P 500, world and US market.
5. Big fee words drifting slowly across the background.

**C · Simpler flow (the outlined phone is clickable)**
- The checks are smaller, and "Zdarma · bez registrace" is gone.
- The result is now just "Rozdíl za 20 let ⓘ", the number, two bars labelled VOO and CSPX with amounts, and "Poslat rozpis e-mailem".
- After submit, the page only confirms: "Rozpis je na cestě".
- The phone switch now reads "Chci pomoct" with "Zavolejte mi a pomozte mi s dalším krokem."

**D · The e-mail.** This is now the actual lead magnet:
- the headline number
- a detailed cost table for both routes
- "Co z toho plyne"
- the assumptions and sources
- a link to a guide on buying the European twin
- the disclaimer and an unsubscribe link

One caution about the help call: it should stick to process help, such as setting up a broker or explaining the calculation. Recommending a specific fund to one person counts as investment advice and needs a licence (ČNB). That's why I avoided "poradit" in the wording.

Send your picks or changes whenever you're ready: which logo, which hero, and anything to adjust in the flow or the e-mail.

<!doctype html>
<html lang="cs">
<head>
<meta charset="utf-8">
<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">
<title>Veenos — photo heroes + cookie consent (v8)</title>
<link rel="preconnect" href="https://fonts.googleapis.com">
<link rel="preconnect" href="https://fonts.gstatic.com" crossorigin>
<link href="https://fonts.googleapis.com/css2?family=Manrope:wght@400;500;600;700;800&display=swap" rel="stylesheet">
<style>
  * { box-sizing: border-box; margin: 0; padding: 0; }
  body { background: #e9e8e5; color: #1b1b1d; font: 15px/1.5 "Manrope", system-ui, sans-serif; padding: 40px 16px 80px; }
  .head, .block { max-width: 1300px; margin: 0 auto; }
  .head { margin-bottom: 26px; }
  .head h1 { font-weight: 800; font-size: 28px; letter-spacing: -0.03em; }
  .head p { max-width: 900px; margin-top: 8px; color: #55555b; }
  .block { margin-bottom: 56px; }
  .block h2 { font-weight: 800; font-size: 20px; letter-spacing: -0.02em; }
  .block .desc { color: #55555b; font-size: 14px; margin: 4px 0 16px; max-width: 900px; }
  .controls { display: flex; flex-wrap: wrap; gap: 18px; margin-bottom: 20px; }
  .ctl { display: flex; gap: 6px; flex-wrap: wrap; align-items: center; }
  .ctl > span { font-size: 12px; font-weight: 700; color: #6b6b70; margin-right: 4px; }
  .ctl button { font: 600 12.5px "Manrope"; border: 1px solid #c9c8c4; background: #fff; border-radius: 999px; padding: 7px 12px; cursor: pointer; }
  .ctl button.on { background: #18191c; color: #fff; border-color: #18191c; }
  .phones { display: flex; gap: 20px; flex-wrap: wrap; align-items: flex-start; }
  .cap { font-size: 12px; color: #4d4d52; text-align: center; margin-top: 8px; font-weight: 700; max-width: 320px; }
  .cap span { display: block; font-weight: 500; color: #7d7d82; }
  .live { outline: 3px solid #cfe68a; outline-offset: 4px; border-radius: 44px; }

  /* tokens + shell */
  .ui { --bg:#18191c; --fg:#f3f3ef; --muted:#a3a49f; --line:#2f3035; --card:#202125; --chip:#26272c; --field:#2c2d32; --accent:#cfe68a; --on-accent:#18191c; font-family: "Manrope", sans-serif; }
  .phone { width: 320px; height: 664px; border-radius: 40px; background: #050505; padding: 9px; box-shadow: 0 16px 36px rgba(0,0,0,.18); flex: none; }
  .frame { position: relative; width: 100%; height: 100%; border-radius: 32px; overflow: hidden; background: var(--bg); color: var(--fg); }

  .bar { position: absolute; top: 0; left: 0; right: 0; z-index: 5; height: 60px; padding: 18px 24px 0; display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: space-between; }
  .wm { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 9px; font-weight: 800; font-size: 20px; letter-spacing: -0.045em; }
  .nav { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 14px; font-size: 13px; font-weight: 600; color: rgba(243,243,239,.8); }
  .burger { width: 38px; height: 38px; border-radius: 50%; border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,.18); display: grid; place-items: center; }
  .burger i, .burger i::before, .burger i::after { display: block; width: 14px; height: 1.6px; background: var(--fg); border-radius: 2px; position: relative; }
  .burger i::before, .burger i::after { content: ""; position: absolute; left: 0; }
  .burger i::before { top: -5px; } .burger i::after { top: 5px; }

  .hero { position: absolute; inset: 0; display: flex; flex-direction: column; padding: 60px 24px 34px; transition: padding .35s cubic-bezier(.2,.8,.2,1); }
  .photo { position: absolute; inset: 0; background: #222 center/cover no-repeat; animation: ken 22s ease-in-out infinite alternate; }
  .scrim { position: absolute; inset: 0; background: linear-gradient(180deg, rgba(24,25,28,.30) 0%, rgba(24,25,28,.30) 28%, rgba(24,25,28,.88) 58%, #18191c 100%); }
  @keyframes ken { from { transform: scale(1); } to { transform: scale(1.1) translate(-6px, 6px); } }
  @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { .photo { animation: none; } }
  .content { position: relative; z-index: 2; margin-top: auto; }

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 21:25:32

v8 has all your v7 changes, plus seven photo options for the hero and the cookie-consent design ([design/previews/v8.html](design/previews/v8.html), commit `91a4adc`).

**Your v7 changes**
- **Logo:** option 1, the V whose right arm rises into an arrow, is now in the header.
- **Headline:** "Jiná" no longer collides with the line above. I increased the line spacing and reduced the size from 44 to 42 px so it stays on two lines.
- **"ukusuje" replaced.** A toggle above the phones switches between three sub-lines:
  - "Zjistěte, kolik vás stojí poplatky za vaše ETF." (default)
  - "Zjistěte, kolik vám z výnosu vezmou poplatky." This one echoes Veenos/výnos.
  - "Spočítejte, kolik zaplatíte za své ETF na poplatcích."
- **Checks:** regular weight now, not bold.
- **Photo credit removed.** The current photos come from Unsplash's free licence, which already allows commercial use without a credit. We'd still confirm each image's licence or buy one before launch.

**A · Seven photo heroes.** The layout is identical in each; only the photo changes:
1. Prague Castle over the river at dusk (the current one)
2. A street lamp with the castle in the distance
3. The castle framed by autumn leaves
4. The St Nicholas dome at sunset
5. Abstract city lights
6. A young man on his phone in a café
7. A woman with her phone

**B · Cookie consent**
- **First visit:** a compact card at the bottom with one short sentence. "Odmítnout" and "Přijmout" are the same size and style, since the Czech data-protection authority (ÚOOÚ) wants rejecting to be as easy as accepting. A "Nastavení" link sits below them.
- **Keeping the hero visible:** while the card shows, the hero moves up and the photo darkens, so the headline and CTA stay readable on the first screen.
- **Settings:** a bottom sheet with Nezbytné (always on), Analytické (Google Analytics) and Marketingové (Meta Pixel). Both are off by default, with "Odmítnout vše", "Přijmout vše" and "Uložit výběr".
- **After a choice:** a small cookie button bottom-left lets the visitor change it later.

Google Analytics and the Meta Pixel load only after consent. Our cookie-free funnel count runs regardless.

Which photo and which sub-line do you prefer, and is the cookie design OK? Any other changes go into the next iteration.

## 👤 User · 2026-10-10 21:34:25

photo 1, sub-line 1, cookie design is fine expect I would make prijmout button highlighted. 

Also, I would love to revisit task line: Cieľom je landing page s porovnaním ETF obchodovaných na NYSE pre českého drobného investora, ktorý prichádza z reklamy na mobile. I feel like we steered away too much from the part with comparing ETF

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 21:35:02

Photo 1, sub-line 1 and the highlighted "Přijmout" are noted. On the highlighted button: it's fine as long as "Odmítnout" stays in the first layer, the same size and clearly readable. The ÚOOÚ treats equal prominence as good practice rather than a hard rule, so this is a small, acceptable risk.

On the brief, I agree, and it's an important catch. The brief asks for "landing page s porovnaním ETF obchodovaných na NYSE". The current page is a cost calculator for two routes to the S&P 500 (VOO via Fio vs CSPX via XTB). Only one NYSE ETF appears, and nothing compares NYSE ETFs side by side. A reviewer could fairly say the comparison is missing.

We drifted because the research found most Czech retail investors can't buy US-domiciled ETFs. They have no PRIIPs KID, so most brokers block them, and only Fio and Patria sell them. That finding is still valuable, but it should be one layer of the comparison, not a replacement for it. The good news is that the headline still fits perfectly: SPY, IVV and VOO track the same S&P 500 but cost 0.0945% vs 0.03% vs 0.03% a year (verified TERs). "Stejný index. Jiná cena." is literally an ETF comparison.

| D13d | Sub-line | **"Zjistěte, kolik vás stojí poplatky za vaše ETF."** | Human choice. |
| D17b | Cookie consent | v8 design approved, with **"Přijmout" highlighted** (accent). "Odmítnout" stays in the first layer, the same size and clearly readable. | Human choice. Lead-agent note: the ÚOOÚ treats equal prominence as good practice, not a hard rule [log D4]. A highlighted accept with an equal-size, visible reject is common; small residual risk. |
| D0b | Scope check | **Human flagged that the concept drifted away from the brief's core: "porovnání ETF obchodovaných na NYSE".** The current page compares two *routes* to one index (VOO via Fio vs CSPX via XTB); it shows no side-by-side comparison of NYSE ETFs. → Concept revisit below. | The brief is the evaluation criterion. The PRIIPs finding (F1) stays, as a layer of the comparison ("can you buy it from CZ / its UCITS twin"), not as its replacement. |
EOF

**→ Bash** — Compute TER-only fee cost per S&P 500 ETF

```
python -I - <<'EOF'
r=(1.07)**(1/12)-1
def fv(m,y,ter=0):
    v=0
    for i in range(y*12): v=v*(1+r)*(1-ter/12)+m
    return v
for m,y in [(3000,20),(5000,20),(3000,30)]:
    base=fv(m,y)
    out={k:round(base-fv(m,y,t),-2) for k,t in [('SPY',0.000945),('IVV',0.0003),('VOO',0.0003),('CSPX',0.0007)]}
    print(m,y,'paid',m*12*y,'base',round(base,-2),out,'SPY-VOO',round(out['SPY']-out['VOO'],-2))
EOF
```

**→ Bash** — Compute route-inclusive costs, monthly vs quarterly Fio orders

```
python -I - <<'EOF'
r=(1.07)**(1/12)-1
USD=21.745
def fv(m,y,ter=0,fx=0,comm=0,every=1):
    v=0; cash=0
    for i in range(y*12):
        v=v*(1+r)*(1-ter/12)
        cash+=m
        if (i+1)%every==0:
            v+=cash*(1-fx)-comm; cash=0
    v+=cash
    return v*(1-fx)
m,y=3000,20
base=fv(m,y)
for every in (1,3):
    res={}
    for k,ter in [('SPY',0.000945),('IVV',0.0003),('VOO',0.0003)]:
        res[k]=round(base-fv(m,y,ter,0.03,7.95*USD,every),-2)
    res['CSPX/XTB']=round(base-fv(m,y,0.0007,0.005,0,1),-2)
    print('Fio buys every',every,'months:',res)
EOF
```

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 21:39:54

v9 is ready ([design/previews/v9.html](design/previews/v9.html), commit `323e139`). It puts the comparison of SPY, IVV and VOO (all NYSE Arca), plus the European twin CSPX, at the centre in four ways.

The same insight shows up in every concept. On the fund's own fee, VOO and IVV are cheapest (0.03%). Once you count buying from Czechia, CSPX wins clearly: 27 900 Kč vs 131 300 Kč over 20 years at 3 000 Kč a month. Most of the gap is Fio's commission and its roughly 3% exchange spread.

- **A · Ranked result:** the approved hero, then amount and years on one screen. The result ranks all four funds by real cost, with badges for "Nejlevnější z ČR" and "Nejnižší poplatek". The comparison only appears after two taps.
- **B · Duel:** the hero asks "VOO, nebo SPY?" with two pickers. A duel card compares fee, cost in CZK and availability from Czechia (VOO vs SPY = 11 900 Kč; VOO vs CSPX = 103 400 Kč). It's the most playful and a strong ad hook, but shows only two funds at a time.
- **C · Calculator + table:** the v8 flow unchanged, with a small table of all four funds below the result. The comparison stays secondary, so it doesn't really fix the problem you raised.
- **D · Comparison on the first screen:** the hero itself shows four fund tiles with their yearly fee, so a visitor sees the NYSE ETF comparison within 2 seconds. "Spočítat v korunách" leads to amount and years, then the ranked result from A.

**My recommendation:** D, with A's ranked result. It's the only concept where the brief's "porovnání ETF z NYSE" is visible before any tap, and the headline "Stejný S&P 500. Jiná cena." works literally. B's duel would make a good second ad creative or A/B challenger rather than the page itself.

The hero's three checks now name the funds ("SPY, IVV a VOO z burzy NYSE"), so the comparison is also clear in A.

Which concept should we take forward, or should I combine elements?

## 👤 User · 2026-10-10 21:43:17

No, I like A. Image #3 with ranked comparision is good idea, but the execution is not good. It feels cluttered, too much happening. Its a quick glance comparision, it should be less complicated, more readable.

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 21:43:32

Restores the brief's core ("porovnání ETF obchodovaných na NYSE"). |

The conclusion holds either way. |

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 21:44:55

Concept A is locked in [research/decisions.md](research/decisions.md), and v10 has four simpler versions of the comparison screen ([design/previews/v10.html](design/previews/v10.html), commit `79dde99`).

I removed the fund cards, badges, exchange and broker sub-labels, fee lines and input chips. What's left is a short context line ("3 000 Kč měsíčně · 20 let ⓘ"), one heading, the four funds with one amount each, the cheapest in lime, and the button. Type is bigger and there's more space. The fees, exchanges, brokers and assumptions go in the e-mail, and the ⓘ opens "Jak počítáme".

1. **Vítěz + seznam:** "Nejlevněji vyjde" above a big **CSPX** and **27 900 Kč**, then a plain list of the NYSE funds below (VOO, IVV, SPY with amounts). The answer comes first.
2. **Sloupce:** "Náklady za 20 let" over four bars on one scale. CSPX's short lime bar against three long grey ones tells the story without reading.
3. **Dvě skupiny:** two blocks only, the European version at 27 900 Kč vs "SPY · IVV · VOO z NYSE" at 131–142 tis. Kč. The simplest read, but it no longer compares the three NYSE funds with each other.
4. **Rozdíl nahoře:** a big "114 200 Kč" (the gap between the cheapest and most expensive fund), then a calm ranked list with a lime dot on the winner.

For a quick-glance comparison I'd go with **2**, or **1** if you want the answer spelled out. Option 3 is the most readable, but it drops the comparison of the NYSE funds with each other, which is the point the brief makes.

Which one, or a mix (e.g. option 1's heading above option 2's bars)?

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 21:49:55

Option 2, the bars, is locked. v11 pulls every decision so far into one clickable page, plus the e-mail ([design/previews/v11.html](design/previews/v11.html), commit `2df2aa0`).

**Flow (the outlined phone is clickable and computes live)**
1. **Cookie card:** "Přijmout" in lime, "Odmítnout" the same size with an outline.
2. **Hero:** logo 1, photo 1, "Stejný index. Jiná cena.", sub-line 1, and three regular-weight checks: "SPY, IVV a VOO z burzy NYSE", "Poplatky v korunách, ne v procentech", "Data od emitentů a ČNB". The CTA A/B toggle is at the top of the preview page.
3. **One card, "Kolik a na jak dlouho?":** monthly amount and years as chips. "Porovnat 4 fondy" stays inactive until both are picked.
4. **Comparison (your option 2):** "Náklady za 20 let", with four bars on one scale for CSPX, IVV, VOO and SPY, the cheapest in lime, then "Poslat srovnání e-mailem".
5. **Form sheet:** name and e-mail, plus "Chci pomoct", which reveals the phone field. An optional checkbox opts into tips.
6. **Confirmation:** "Srovnání je na cestě".
7. **Bottom of the page:** "Jak počítáme", then the footer with the disclaimer and a "Nastavení cookies" link.

**E-mail:** a subject with the visitor's inputs, then the same bar chart. A "Z čeho se náklady skládají" table splits each fund's cost into the fund fee and buying from Czechia. A short "Co z toho plyne", a "Jak koupit CSPX u svého brokera" button, then assumptions, sources and the disclaimer.

**Two small changes I made along the way:**
- I removed the floating cookie button because it covered page text. Visitors can change their choice through the "Nastavení cookies" footer link instead, which is the usual, compliant way.
- "Poslat celé srovnání e-mailem" was too wide for the button and is now "Poslat srovnání e-mailem".

If v11 looks right, the next step is the build plan: stack, hosting on Vercel, form backend, e-mail delivery, tracking and ad creatives. Otherwise, tell me what to change.

P=/c/Users/matus/Desktop/B_project/design/previews; python -I - "$P/v12.html" <<'EOF'
import sys
p=sys.argv[1]; s=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()
old_shot = """function shot(src, w, h, s, extraStyle = '') {
  return `<div class="shot" style="width:${Math.round(w * s)}px;height:${Math.round(h * s)}px;${extraStyle}"><iframe loading="lazy" src="${src}" width="${w}" height="${h}" style="transform:scale(${s})" title="náhled"></iframe></div>`;
}"""
new_shot = """// The site and the e-mail are inlined (window.SITE / window.EMAIL) so the frames also work when this page is shown as a snapshot.
function shot(src, w, h, s, extraStyle = '') {
  const q = src.split('?')[1] || '';
  return `<div class="shot" style="width:${Math.round(w * s)}px;height:${Math.round(h * s)}px;${extraStyle}"><iframe data-doc="site" name="${q}" width="${w}" height="${h}" style="transform:scale(${s})" title="náhled"></iframe></div>`;
}"""
assert old_shot in s
s=s.replace(old_shot,new_shot)
s=s.replace("""const add = (id, html, cap, sub, href) => document.getElementById(id).insertAdjacentHTML('beforeend', `<div>${html}<div class="cap">${cap}<span>${sub}${href ? ` · <a href="${href}" target="_blank">open</a>` : ''}</span></div></div>`);""",
"""const add = (id, html, cap, sub) => document.getElementById(id).insertAdjacentHTML('beforeend', `<div>${html}<div class="cap">${cap}<span>${sub}</span></div></div>`);""")
s=s.replace('<iframe src="v12-email.html" width="390"','<iframe data-doc="email" width="390"')
s=s.replace('<iframe src="v12-email.html" width="900"','<iframe data-doc="email" width="900"')
s=s.replace('(<a href="v12-site.html">v12-site.html</a>)','(<code>v12-site.html</code>)')
s=s.replace(' <a href="v12-email.html">Open the e-mail on its own</a>.',' File: <code>v12-email.html</code>.')
# fill frames after building
s=s.replace("</script>\n</body>","document.querySelectorAll('iframe[data-doc]').forEach(f => { f.srcdoc = f.dataset.doc === 'email' ? window.EMAIL : window.SITE; });\n</script>\n</body>")
# inline payload slot before main script
s=s.replace("<script>\nconst AV","<script id=\"inline\">/*INLINE*/</script>\n<script>\nconst AV",1)
open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(s)
print('ok', s.count('data-doc'))
EOF
cat > /c/Users/matus/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-matus-Desktop-B-project/66014a9f-a5a6-413b-8552-f9844a0020a6/scratchpad/inline_v12.py <<'EOF'
"""Inline v12-site.html and v12-email.html into v12.html (re-runnable)."""
import json, re, sys
from pathlib import Path
d = Path(sys.argv[1])
site = (d / 'v12-site.html').read_text(encoding='utf-8')
email = (d / 'v12-email.html').read_text(encoding='utf-8')
show = d / 'v12.html'
s = show.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
payload = 'window.SITE=' + json.dumps(site).replace('</', '<\\/') + ';window.EMAIL=' + json.dumps(email).replace('</', '<\\/') + ';'
s = re.sub(r'<script id="inline">.*?</script>', lambda m: '<script id="inline">' + payload + '</script>', s, count=1, flags=re.S)
show.write_text(s, encoding='utf-8')
print('inlined', len(site), len(email))
EOF

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 22:00:43

The sed copy failed, so an empty script got committed and the showcase wasn't regenerated. Fixing that properly.

**→ Write** `C:\Users\matus\Desktop\B_project\scripts\inline_v12_preview.py`

```
"""Inline design/previews/v12-site.html and v12-email.html into v12.html.

The device showcase (v12.html) renders the site and the e-mail in iframes via `srcdoc`,
so it also works when the page is viewed as a single file/snapshot. Re-run after editing
either source file:

  python -I scripts/inline_v12_preview.py design/previews
"""
import json
import re
import sys
from pathlib import Path


def js_string(text):
    # JSON-encode and escape "</" so the payload cannot close the <script> tag early.
    return json.dumps(text).replace("</", "<\\/")


def main(folder):
    d = Path(folder)
    site = (d / "v12-site.html").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    email = (d / "v12-email.html").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    show = d / "v12.html"
    page = show.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
    payload = "window.SITE=" + js_string(site) + ";window.EMAIL=" + js_string(email) + ";"
    page, n = re.subn(r'<script id="inline">.*?</script>', lambda m: '<script id="inline">' + payload + "</script>", page, count=1, flags=re.S)
    if n != 1:
        sys.exit("inline slot not found in v12.html")
    show.write_text(page, encoding="utf-8")
    print(f"inlined site ({len(site)} chars) and e-mail ({len(email)} chars) into {show}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main(sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "design/previews")
```

### 🤖 Claude · 2026-10-10 22:01:08

v12 is ready ([design/previews/v12.html](design/previews/v12.html), commit `7bc38f2`). It's no longer phone mockups: it's one real responsive page ([v12-site.html](design/previews/v12-site.html)), shown in phone, tablet and desktop frames, and every frame is clickable.

**Variant B** uses the young man with the phone and "Spočítat poplatky"; variant A keeps Prague Castle and "Začít investovat". Because B changes both the photo and the CTA, the test will show whether the "younger" variant as a whole converts better, not which of the two changes did it. I've noted that for the README's A/B hypotheses; a follow-up test could separate them.

**Mobile (390 px):** the flow from v11, with the same cookie card, calculator, bars and bottom-sheet form.

**Tablet (768 px):** the same order as mobile with more room. The headline is 64 px, the CTA is a pill rather than full-width, the calculator is capped at 620 px and "Jak počítáme" is in two columns. The form and cookie settings open as a centred dialog, and the cookie card sits bottom-left.

**Desktop (1440 px):**
- The header shows full navigation (Srovnání · Jak počítáme · Zdroje dat · O nás) and a "Spočítat" pill.
- The hero puts the headline on the left and the calculator card on the right, so the comparison is one click away without scrolling.
- After "Porovnat 4 fondy", the bars replace the card in place, with "← Změnit zadání" to go back.
- "Jak počítáme" is four tiles, and the footer has three columns.

**E-mail ([v12-email.html](design/previews/v12-email.html))**
- **Format:** built like a real e-mail (table layout, 600 px wide) and shown both in a mobile mail app and a desktop mail client.
- **Inbox line:** subject "Vaše srovnání S&P 500 ETF: CSPX vychází nejlevněji", with a short preview line beside it.
- **Look:** the site's language: a dark header band, the V-arrow logo, "Vaše srovnání / S&P 500 ETF" in lime, rounded cards and a lime pill button.
- **Copy:**
  - greeting and a short intro
  - "Nejlevněji vám z Česka vyjde: CSPX 27 900 Kč", with the four bars
  - "Z čeho se náklady skládají", with issuer, exchange and broker per fund
  - "Co z toho plyne" in four points
  - a dark "Brzy vám zavoláme" box, only when "Chci pomoct" was on
  - the button and "Hezký den, Tým Veenos"
  - assumptions and sources, and the legal footer with "Odhlásit"
- **Button wording:** I set it to "Návod: jak koupit ETF u brokera" rather than "Jak koupit CSPX". A personal "buy this fund" call to action would lean towards an investment recommendation (the ČNB line), so the neutral version is safer.

The footer shows "Veenos · Praha · srovnani@veenos.cz" as a stand-in. The real operator details (company and registration number, IČO) need filling in before launch, and I didn't make any up.

The responsive file is a working page rather than a mockup, so it can become the base for the actual build. If the design is close to final, the next step is the build plan: stack, Vercel, form backend, e-mail sending, tracking and ad creatives.
